) is to partition the energy spectrum into a set of non-overlapping energy intervals
\{S_{i}\}_{i}
(Fig.
11
) using the projectors

\displaystyle\Pi_{S_{i}}\coloneq\sum_{E_{i}\in[i\Delta_{\rm RMT},(i+1)\Delta_{\rm RMT}]}\ket{E_i}\bra{E_i}.

(157)

We start with the terms involving
A^{*}\otimes A
. For
\Delta K_{A\otimes A}\coloneq\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\mu+\nu)}((A^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{a}_{\mu}-\mathbb{E}_{R}[(A^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{a}_{\mu}])
and an even integer
p
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{\infty}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{\infty}^{p}\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{\bar{E}_{1}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:sum_gaussian}}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\frac{1}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\mu+\nu)}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}((A^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{a}_{\mu}-(A^{\prime a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{\prime a}_{\mu})(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:B_gaussian}}{=}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\frac{2}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\mu+\nu)}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}(B^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes B^{\prime a}_{\mu}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:G_variance}}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\frac{2}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}G^{a}_{S_{k},S_{i}}\otimes G^{\prime a}_{S_{l},S_{j}}\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:decouple_gaussian}}{\leq}\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G_{S_{k},S_{i}}\|_{p}^{p}\,\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G_{S_{l},S_{j}}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_p_norm}}{\leq}\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)^{p}\left(\max\{\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{k}}],\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{i}}]\}\mathbb{E}_{R}[|G_{S_{k},S_{i}}|^{2}]\max\{\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{l}}],\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{j}}]\}\mathbb{E}_{R}[|G_{S_{l},S_{j}}|^{2}]\right)^{p/2}.

(158)

In the third line, we used that, for the zero-mean random matrix
X\coloneq(A^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{a}_{\mu}-\mathbb{E}[(A^{a}_{\nu})^{*}\otimes A^{a}_{\mu}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\|X\|_{p}^{p}\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\|X-X^{\prime}\|_{p}^{p},

(159)

where
X
and
X^{\prime}
are i.i.d. random matrices (all the diagonal entries are zero).
In the fourth line, we wrote
A=(B+B^{\prime})/\sqrt{2}
and
A^{\prime}=(B-B^{\prime})/\sqrt{2}
with zero-mean random matrices
B
and
B^{\prime}
. Then, it follows

\displaystyle A^{*}\otimes A-A^{\prime*}\otimes A^{\prime}=B^{*}\otimes B^{\prime}+B^{\prime*}\otimes B.

(160)

In the fifth line, we defined the zero-mean random matrices
G_{\bar{F},\bar{E}}
whose entries all have the same variance,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}[|(G_{S^{\prime},S}^{a})_{ij}|^{2}]=\max_{F\in S^{\prime},E\in S}(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(F-E)}\eta_{F-E})^{2}\mathbb{E}_{R}[|(B^{a}_{F-E})_{ij}|^{2}]=\max_{F\in S^{\prime},E\in S}(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(F-E)}\eta_{F-E})^{2}\frac{|f(F-E)|^{2}}{D\big(\frac{F+E}{2}\big)}.

(161)

In the sixth line, the Gaussian matrices are decoupled as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{R}\Big\|\sum_{a}c_{a}G^{a}\otimes G^{\prime a}\Big\|_{p}^{p}\\
&=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}c_{a_{1}}c_{a_{2}}^{*}\dots c_{a_{p-1}}c_{a_{p}}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}]\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{\prime a_{1}}G^{\prime a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{\prime a_{p-1}}G^{\prime a_{p}{\dagger}}]\\
&\leq\big(\sum_{a}|c_{a}|^{2}\big)^{p/2}\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}]\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{\prime a_{1}}G^{\prime a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{\prime a_{p-1}}G^{\prime a_{p}{\dagger}}]\\
&\leq\big(\sum_{a}|c_{a}|^{2}\big)^{p/2}\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}]\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{b_{1},\dots,b_{p}}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{\prime b_{1}}G^{\prime b_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{\prime b_{p-1}}G^{\prime b_{p}{\dagger}}]\\
&\leq\big(\sum_{a}|c_{a}|^{2}\big)^{p/2}\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G\|_{p}^{p}\,\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G^{\prime}\|_{p}^{p}.\end{split}

(162)

The quantity
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{\prime b_{1}}\dots G^{\prime b_{p}{\dagger}}]
is non-vanishing upon averaging over independent random matrices
R
or equivalently
G^{a}
only when all the indices make pairs. Accordingly, the sum
\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}
only runs over such indices.
The last inequality holds because
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}]\geq 0
holds when the indices are contracted.
The seventh line follows from Fact D.1 in
[
60
]
. For a
d_{1}\times d_{2}
matrix
G
of the maximum variance
\Var_{G}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G\|_{p}^{p}\leq\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}\Var_{G}^{p/2}\big(\big(c_{1}\sqrt{\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}\big)^{p}+(c_{2}\sqrt{p})^{p}\big),

(163)

for constants
c_{1},c_{2}
.
Setting
p
such that
\min\{d_{1},d_{2}\}^{1/p}\leq 1/c_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G\|_{p}^{p})^{1/p}\leq\sqrt{\Var_{G}\cdot\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}}.

(164)

Therefore, we can upper-bound the fluctuation in each eigensector,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sqrt{\max_{E\in S_{k}\cup S_{i}}D(E)\max_{F\in S_{l}\cup S_{j}}D(F)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\max_{E_{k}\in S_{k},E_{i}\in S_{i}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(E_{k}-E_{i})}\eta_{E_{k}-E_{i}}|f(E_{k}-E_{i})|}{\sqrt{D\big(\frac{E_{k}+E_{i}}{2}\big)}}\max_{E_{l}\in S_{l},E_{j}\in S_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(E_{l}-E_{j})}\eta_{E_{l}-E_{j}}|f(E_{l}-E_{j})|}{\sqrt{D\big(\frac{E_{l}+E_{j}}{2}\big)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{k}-\hat{E}_{i})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{k}-\hat{E}_{i}}|f(\hat{E}_{k}-\hat{E}_{i})|\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{l}-\hat{E}_{j})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{l}-\hat{E}_{j}}|f(\hat{E}_{l}-\hat{E}_{j})|,

(165)

where
\hat{E}_{k},\hat{E}_{i}
is the pair of energies solving the first maximization of the third line, and
\hat{E}_{l},\hat{E}_{j}
solves the second maximization of the third line.
Recalling Assumption
2
, for the second line we used that the number of states in each energy interval can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{i}}]\leq D(E_{i})R_{D}\Delta_{\rm RMT},\quad\text{for all }E_{i}\in S_{i}.

(166)

The last line in Eq. (
165
) holds only if
\hat{E}_{i}\in S_{i}
and
\hat{E}_{k}\in S_{k}
are close, which is true because
f(\hat{E}_{k}-\hat{E}_{i})
vanishes for
|\hat{E}_{k}-\hat{E}_{i}|>\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}
(Assumption
1
(a)
). Same for
\hat{E}_{j}
and
\hat{E}_{l}
.

Now, we add all the energy eigensectors.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\|\Delta K_{A\otimes A}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\sum_{i,j,k,l}(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,n}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\sum_{i,j}(\Pi_{S_{i+m}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j+n}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,n}\max_{i,j}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|(\Pi_{S_{i+m}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j+n}})\Delta K_{A\otimes A}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip-6.00006pt\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_local_AxA}}{\leq}\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\Big(\sum_{m}\max_{i}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i}}|f(\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i})|\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\Big(\sum_{m}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}}}|f(\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\imath}})|\Big)^{2}

(167)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\Big(\sum_{m}\mathrm{e}^{\beta\hat{\nu}_{m}/4}\eta_{\hat{\nu}_{m}}|f(\hat{\nu}_{m})|\Big)^{2}

(168)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.0pt\overset{\text{Ass.}\ref{as:dos_ratio}}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\nu\,\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu/4}\eta_{\nu}|f(\nu)|\right)^{2}\right)\qquad\text{where we used }\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sum_{m}\approx\int\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-6.00006pt\overset{\eqref{eq:CSineq_integral}}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right).

(169)

In the inequality (
167
), we defined
\hat{\imath}(m)\coloneq\underset{i}{\rm argmax}\,\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i}}|f(\hat{E}_{i+m}-\hat{E}_{i})|
, and suppressed its
m
-dependence for the notational simplicity.
In the inequality (
168
),
\hat{\nu}_{m}
is given by
\nu_{m}\in\bigcup_{i}\{E_{i+m}-E_{i}|E_{i}\in S_{i},E_{i+m}\in S_{i+m}\}
that maximizes
\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu_{m}/4}\eta_{\nu_{m}}|f(\nu_{m})|
.
The last equality follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\nu\,\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu/4}\eta_{\nu}|f(\nu)|\right)^{2}\leq\underbrace{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\nu\,\eta_{\nu}^{2}}_{=1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\nu^{\prime}\,\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu^{\prime}/2}|f(\nu^{\prime})|^{2}\overset{\eqref{eq:f_scale_derivation}}{=}\mathcal{O}(1).

(170)

Next, we bound the norm of
\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}\coloneq\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}I\otimes\big((A^{a}_{\mu})^{\dagger}A^{a}_{\nu}-\mathbb{E}_{R}[(A^{a}_{\mu})^{\dagger}A^{a}_{\nu}]\big)
.

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{\infty}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{S_{k}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(\Pi_{S_{i}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\Pi_{S_{l}}\frac{1}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\beta}{4}(\mu-\nu)}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}\big((A^{a}_{\mu})^{\dagger}A^{a}_{\nu}-(A^{\prime a}_{\mu})^{\dagger}A^{\prime a}_{\nu}\big)\Pi_{S_{j}}\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\frac{2}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\beta}{4}(\mu-\nu)}\eta_{\mu}\eta_{\nu}\Pi_{S_{l}}(B^{a}_{\mu})^{\dagger}B^{\prime a}_{\nu}\Pi_{S_{j}}\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\frac{2}{|{\bm{A}}|}\sum_{a}\sum_{h}G_{S_{l},S_{h}}G_{S_{h},S_{j}}\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:decouple_gaussian_IxAA}}{\leq}\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)^{p}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\sum_{h}G_{S_{l},S_{h}}G_{S_{h},S_{j}}\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:p_to_infty_norm}}{\leq}\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)^{p}\Big(\sum_{h}\max\{\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{l}}],\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{h}}]\}\mathbb{E}_{R}[|G_{S_{l},S_{h}}|^{2}]\max\{\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{h}}],\mathrm{Tr}[\Pi_{S_{j}}]\}\mathbb{E}_{R}[|G_{S_{h},S_{j}}|^{2}]\Big)^{p/2}.

(171)

In the second last inequality, the Gaussian matrices are decoupled as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{R}\Big\|\sum_{a}c_{a}G^{a}G^{\prime a}\Big\|_{p}^{p}\\
&=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}c_{a_{1}}c_{a_{2}}^{*}\dots c_{a_{p-1}}c_{a_{p}}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{\prime a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}G^{\prime a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{\prime a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}G^{\prime a_{p}{\dagger}}]\\
&\leq\big(\sum_{a}|c_{a}|^{2}\big)^{p/2}\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{\prime a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}G^{\prime a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{\prime a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}G^{\prime a_{p}{\dagger}}]\\
&=\big(\sum_{a}|c_{a}|^{2}\big)^{p/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{R}\|G^{1}G^{\prime 1}\|_{p}^{p}\big)^{2}.\end{split}

(172)

The sum
\sum^{\text{pairs}}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}
only runs over the indices that consist only of pairs.
The last inequality holds because
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathrm{Tr}[G^{a_{1}}G^{a_{2}{\dagger}}\dots G^{a_{p-1}}G^{a_{p}{\dagger}}]\geq 0
holds when all the indices are paired.
The last inequality follows from,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|GG^{\prime}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{2}\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|G\|_{\infty}^{p}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|G^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{p}\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_p_norm}}{\leq}\sqrt{\Var_{G}\Var_{G^{\prime}}\cdot\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}\max\{d_{2},d_{3}\}},

(173)

for a
d_{1}\times d_{2}
matrix
G
and a
d_{2}\times d_{3}
matrix
G^{\prime}
. The even integer
p
is chosen so that
d_{2}^{1/p}\leq 1
.

Therefore, we can upper-bound the fluctuation in each eigensector,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\big\|(\Pi_{\bar{F}_{1}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(\Pi_{\bar{E}_{1}}\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sum_{S_{h}}\sqrt{\max_{E\in S_{l}\cup S_{h}}D(E)\max_{F\in S_{h}\cup S_{j}}D(F)}

\displaystyle\times\max_{E_{l}\in S_{l},E_{h}\in S_{h}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(E_{l}-E_{h})}\eta_{E_{l}-E_{h}}\big|f\big(\frac{E_{l}+E_{h}}{2},E_{l}-E_{h}\big)\big|}{\sqrt{D\big(\frac{E_{l}+E_{h}}{2}\big)}}\max_{E_{h}\in S_{h},E_{j}\in S_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(E_{h}-E_{j})}\eta_{E_{h}-E_{j}}\big|f\big(\frac{E_{h}+E_{j}}{2},E_{h}-E_{j}\big)\big|}{\sqrt{D\big(\frac{E_{h}+E_{j}}{2}\big)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sum_{h}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{l}-\hat{E}_{h})}\eta_{\hat{E_{l}}-\hat{E}_{h}}|f(\hat{E}_{l}-\hat{E}_{h})|\ \mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j}}|f(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j})|,

(174)

where
\hat{E}_{h},\hat{E}_{j},\hat{E}_{l}
are the energies solving the maximization of the third line.

Summing the contributions from all the energy eigenspaces, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\|\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\sum_{j,l}(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{l}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|\sum_{j}(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{j+m}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m}\max_{j}\mathbb{E}_{R}\left\|(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{j+m}})\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}(I\otimes\Pi_{S_{j}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip-6.00006pt\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_local_IxAA}}{\leq}\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sum_{m,h}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+m}-\hat{E}_{h})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+m}-\hat{E}_{h}}|f(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+m}-\hat{E}_{h})|\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}}}|f(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}})|

(175)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sum_{m,h}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h}}|f(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h+m}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h})|\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}}}|f(\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}+h}-\hat{E}_{\hat{\jmath}})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\rm RMT}R_{D}^{2}}{\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\sum_{h}\mathrm{e}^{\beta\hat{\nu}_{m}/4}\eta_{\hat{\nu}_{m}}|f(\hat{\nu}_{m})|\sum_{m}\mathrm{e}^{\beta\hat{\nu}_{h}/4}\eta_{\hat{\nu}_{h}}|f(\hat{\nu}_{h})|

(176)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.0pt\overset{\text{Ass.}\ref{as:dos_ratio}}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\nu\,\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu/4}\eta_{\nu}|f(\nu)|\right)^{2}\right)\qquad\text{where we used }\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sum_{h}\approx\int\mathrm{d}\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-6.00006pt\overset{\eqref{eq:CSineq_integral}}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right).

(177)

In the inequality (
175
),
\hat{\jmath}(m)
solves the maximization,
\hat{\jmath}(m)\coloneq\underset{j}{\rm argmax}\,\sum_{h}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{j+m}-\hat{E}_{h})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{j+m}-\hat{E}_{h}}|f(\hat{E}_{j+m}-\hat{E}_{h})|\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta}{4}(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j})}\eta_{\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j}}|f(\hat{E}_{h}-\hat{E}_{j})|
, and its
m
-dependence is suppressed for the notational simplicity.
In the inequality (
176
),
\hat{\nu}_{x}
for an integer
x
is given by
\nu_{x}\in\bigcup_{i}\{E_{i+x}-E_{i}|E_{i}\in S_{i},E_{i+x}\in S_{i+x}\}
that maximizes
\mathrm{e}^{\beta\nu_{x}/4}\eta_{\nu_{x}}|f(\nu_{x})|
.

Similarly, one can obtain the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\|\Delta K_{AA\otimes I}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right).

(178)

Overall, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{R}\|{\cal D}-\mathbb{E}_{R}{\cal D}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2\to 2}&=\mathbb{E}_{R}\|\Delta K_{A\otimes A}+\Delta K_{I\otimes AA}+\Delta K_{AA\otimes I}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)\end{split}

(179)

Furthermore, a careful concentration analysis in
[
60
]
together with Eq. (
151
) shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|{\mathcal{L}}-\mathbb{E}_{R}{\mathcal{L}}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2\to 2}=\|{\mathcal{D}}-\mathbb{E}_{R}{\mathcal{D}}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2\to 2}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right)\end{split}

(180)

holds with the probability exponentially close to 1 with respect to
n
.
Using
\Delta_{\rm RMT}=\Theta(1/\beta)
, we can simplify Eq. (
180
) to

\|\mathcal{L}-\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2\to 2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\beta}{\sqrt{|\bm{A}|}}\right).

(181)

C.5
Bounding the convergence to the Gibbs state

In the previous sections
C.3
and
C.4
we showed that the gap of the average Lindbladian
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}
is lower-bounded by a polynomial in
\beta
and the system size
n
, whereas the channel distance between the actual Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}
and its average
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}
is upper-bounded by the inverse square root of the number of jump operators
|\bm{A}|
. In this section we will use both results to polynomially bound the spectral gap of the true Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}
and to show a high convergence accuracy of the Lindblad evolution, in the sense that its exact steady state
\rho_{\infty}
is close to the target Gibbs state
\sigma_{\beta}
, which is the steady state of
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}
.

Let us start with the spectral gap.
From the gap
\Delta_{\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}}
of the ETH-averaged Lindbladian (
149
) and the channel distance (
181
), we obtain a lower bound on the gap
\Delta_{\cal L}
of the Lindbladian
{\cal L}=\mathbb{E}_{R}{\cal L}+({\cal L}-\mathbb{E}_{R}{\cal L})
via

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}=\Omega\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\beta\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}}\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}^{2}\Gamma}{\Delta_{\mathrm{spec}}^{2}}\right)-\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\rm RMT}\sqrt{|{\bm{A}}|}}\right).

(182)

Hence, there exists a number of jump operators,

\displaystyle|{\bm{A}}|=\Omega\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{4\beta\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}}\Delta_{\mathrm{spec}}^{4}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}^{6}\Gamma^{2}}\right),

(183)

for which the spectral gap (
182
) is lower bounded by

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}=\Omega\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-2\beta\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}}\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}^{2}\Gamma}{\Delta_{\mathrm{spec}}^{2}}\right),

(184)

with high probability.
Finally, using Eq. (
16
) and
\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma_{\beta}^{-1}\|_{\infty})\approx\beta\|H\|_{\infty}
, the lower bound of the spectral gap is turned into an upper bound of the mixing time,

\displaystyle t_{\mathrm{mix}}\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\Big(\beta\|H\|_{\infty}+\mathrm{log}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{2\beta\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}}\Delta_{\mathrm{spec}}^{2}}{\Delta_{\mathrm{RMT}}^{2}\Gamma}\right).

(185)

With Assumption
1
(b)
,
\Delta_{\rm spec}\propto 1/\sqrt{n}
, spectral gap (
184
), the sufficient number of jump operators (
183
), the mixing time (
185
), and the bounds on the overlap integral
\Gamma
(
114
) and (
113
), we find

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}=\Omega\left(\frac{1}{n\beta^{2}}\right),\qquad|{\bm{A}}|=\Omega(n^{2}\beta^{6}),\qquad t_{\mathrm{mix}}=\mathcal{O}\left(n\beta^{2}\big(\beta\|H\|_{\infty}+\mathrm{log}(1/\epsilon)\big)\right).

(186)

As a reminder, we used the assumption of ETH (
107
) and Assumptions
1
,
2
, and
3
to derive the spectral gap.
This proves Eq. (
106
) of Thm.
2
.

Let us now come to the distance between Gibbs state and steady state of
\mathcal{L}
.
The inequality (
180
) allows us to bound the
\chi^{2}
divergence,
\|\sigma_{\beta}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2}
, between Gibbs state and the steady state
\rho_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{L}
, which in turn, bounds the trace distance,
\|\sigma_{\beta}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{1}\leq\|\sigma_{\beta}-\rho_{\infty}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2}

[
56
]
.
Let us simplify notation,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathcal{L}
, and let
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
be states such that
\mathcal{L}[\sigma]=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}]=0
.
Then

\displaystyle\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}=\|\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}[\sigma]-\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}[\sigma^{\prime}]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}\leq\|\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}[\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}+\|(\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}-\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})[\sigma^{\prime}]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}.

(187)

To bound the second term, we note

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}-\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}&\leq t\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\|\mathrm{e}^{st\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime})\mathrm{e}^{(1-s)t\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\\
&\leq t\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\|\mathrm{e}^{st\mathcal{L}}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\cdot\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\cdot\|\mathrm{e}^{(1-s)t\mathcal{L}}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\\
&\leq t\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2},\end{split}

(188)

where in the last inequality we used

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{x\mathcal{L}}[\rho]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}=\|\mathrm{e}^{x\mathcal{L}}[\rho-\sigma]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}+1\leq\|\rho-\sigma\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}+1=\|\rho\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}

(189)

for
x\geq 0
, leading to

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{x\mathcal{L}}[\rho]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}=\max_{\rho}\frac{\|\mathrm{e}^{x\mathcal{L}}[\rho]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}}{\|\rho\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}}\leq 1.

(190)

The same inequality holds for
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.
Thus, the second term in Eq. (
187
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}-\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})[\sigma^{\prime}]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}\leq t\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\cdot\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}.

(191)

Note that
\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}
can be evaluated as follows:

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}\leq\|\sigma\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}+\|\sigma^{\prime}-\sigma\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}=1+\|\sigma^{\prime}-\sigma\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}.

(192)

To bound the first term in (
187
), we set
t=\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma^{-1}\|_{\infty}/\epsilon)
for the spectral gap
\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}
of
\mathcal{L}
(
184
) so that the following inequality holds,

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{t\mathcal{L}}[\sigma^{\prime}-\sigma]\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}\leq\mathrm{e}^{-t\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}}\|\sigma^{\prime}-\sigma\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}\leq\epsilon

(193)

Putting these together, we have the bound on (
187
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}&\leq\epsilon+t\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}(1+\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2})\\
&\leq\frac{\epsilon+\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma^{-1}\|_{\infty}/\epsilon)\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}}{1-\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma^{-1}\|_{\infty}/\epsilon)\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}},\end{split}

(194)

which also upper bounds the trace distance due to
\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq\|\sigma-\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2}
.

Setting
\sigma^{\prime}=\rho_{\infty}
,
\sigma=\sigma_{\beta}
, and using Eqs. (
181
) and (
186
),
we can bound

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma^{-1}\|_{\infty}/\epsilon)\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{n\beta^{3}}{\sqrt{|\bm{A}|}}\left(\beta\|H\|_{\infty}+\mathrm{log}(1/\epsilon)\right)\right),

(195)

where we used
\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma_{\beta}^{-1}\|_{\infty})\approx\beta\|H\|_{\infty}
.
With this, we obtain the following upper bound on the trace distance,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\rho_{\infty}-\sigma_{\beta}\|_{1}&\leq\|\rho_{\infty}-\sigma_{\beta}\|_{\sigma_{\beta}^{-1},2}\overset{\mathrm{w.h.p}}{\leq}\epsilon+\big(\Delta_{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\mathrm{log}(\|\sigma_{\beta}^{-1}\|_{\infty}/\epsilon)\|\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\|_{\sigma^{-1},2\to 2}\\
&=\epsilon+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{n\beta^{3}}{\sqrt{|\bm{A}|}}\left(\beta\|H\|_{\infty}+\mathrm{log}(1/\epsilon)\right)\right)=2\epsilon.\end{split}

(196)

This proves Eq. (
105
) in Thm.
2
.
The second inequality holds with a high probability.
In the last equality, we set the number of jump operators as

\displaystyle|{\bm{A}}|=\Omega\left(\frac{n^{2}\beta^{8}\|H\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\epsilon}\right),

(197)

so that
\rho_{\infty}
is
2\epsilon
-close to
\sigma_{\beta}
in trace distance. Choosing the number of jump operators as Eq. (
197
) automatically guarantees the second equation in Eq. (
186
).

Appendix D
Details on numerical experiments and further results

We discuss further numerical results for the mixing time and convergence accuracy of the Lindblad dynamics (
2
). We expand the analysis of Sec.
5
by investigating the impact of the locality
k
of the jump operators, and of the initial state in App.
D.2
.
Furthermore, we extend our analysis to more parameter points, exploring further regular limits and the edge of chaos of the model (
30
).
We start this section by giving a more detailed overview of fractal dimensions and eigenstate delocalization, from which we identify the various dynamical regimes of our mixed-field Ising model (
30
).
In App.
D.3
, we give further details on our Lindbladian simulation scheme.
Throughout this section we fix
\beta=(2J)^{-1}
, as in the main text.

D.1
Eigenstate delocalization

Figure 12:

Fractal dimension analysis of the mixed-field Ising model (
30
) for
n=8
qubits. The upper and lower rows show, respectively, mean
\mathbb{E}[D_{1}]
and variance
\text{Var}[D_{1}]
of the first fractal dimension, Eq. (
198
), evaluated over the inner
80\%
of the spectrum, as a function of transverse and longitudinal field strengths.
The four columns correspond to four different Pauli strings (given in each column’s title) which define the basis in which the fractal dimension is evaluated.
Large mean values
\mathbb{E}[D_{1}]

in conjunction with small
\text{Var}[D_{1}]
are a signature of quantum chaos.
We identify a clear region of uniform eigenstate delocalization below the center (
m=h=1
) of the phase diagrams, which is present for all considered Pauli bases.
Within the phase diagram, we identify eight parameter choices (white crosses), with broadly varying eigenstate characteristics, of interest, which we will consider in more depth in the following convergence analysis. They are summarized in Tab.
1
.

Eigenstate thermalization is expected to accurately describe the behavior of quantum chaotic systems
[
48
]
.
Typical signatures of quantum chaos comprise, for example, spectral statistics
[
85
,
86
]
or the delocalization behavior of energy eigenstates
[
73
,
74
,
106
,
107
,
108
,
75
,
76
]
.
We identify sets of parameters
(h,m)
of the mixed-field Ising model (
30
) with distinct quantum chaotic properties by following the approach advocated in
[
75
,
76
]
, based on delocalization of energy eigenstates.
This approach directly assesses the crucial properties underlying the ETH, while simultaneously being independent of a specific choice of observables, i.e. of the Lindbladian jump operators in our case.

D.1.1
Eigenstate delocalization and fractal dimension

Quantum chaos is signaled by a strong and uniform delocalization of a substantial part of the energy eigenstates in almost any basis of the Hilbert space (excluding pathological cases such as the eigenbasis itself)
[
73
,
74
,
106
,
107
,
108
,
75
,
76
]
.
A way to quantify eigenstate delocalization in a given Hilbert space basis
\ket{\zeta}
is via so-called fractal dimensions.
For an eigenstate
\ket{E_i}=\sum_{\zeta=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}}\psi_{\zeta}^{i}\ket{\zeta}
of (
30
) the finite-size generalized fractal dimensions are defined by

D_{q}^{(i)}=\frac{1}{1-q}\mathrm{log}_{\dim\mathcal{H}}R_{q}^{(i)}\,,\quad R_{q}^{(i)}=\sum_{\zeta=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}}|\psi^{i}_{\zeta}|^{2q}\,,\quad q\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(198)

where
\dim\mathcal{H}
is the dimension of the Hilbert space.
In the following we will omit the superscript
(i)
of
D_{q}
if it is not necessary.
For
q=1
the definition is given by taking the limit,
D_{1}=\lim_{q\rightarrow 1}D_{q}
, which coincides with the normalized Shannon entropy of the probability distribution
\{|\psi_{\zeta}|^{2}\}_{\zeta}
.
In the large-system limit one can identify three different regimes: localized (the eigenstate is supported only on a vanishingly small portion of basis states, with
\lim_{\dim\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\infty}D_{q}=0
for all
q\geq 1
), multifractal or extended non-ergodic (
0\leq\lim_{\dim\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\infty}D_{q}\leq 1
dependent on
q
) and ergodic (
\lim_{\dim\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\infty}D_{q}=1
for all
q
, which holds for the limit of the uniform distribution
|\psi^{i}_{\zeta}|^{2}=1/\dim\mathcal{H}
for all
\zeta
).

For uniform eigenstate delocalization in a given expansion basis, two statistical signatures are crucial: Firstly, a large average fractal dimension
\mathbb{E}[D_{q}]
, where the average
\mathbb{E}
is taken over the eigenstates
\{\ket{E_i}\}_{i}
, indicates that almost all eigenstates are strongly delocalized. Secondly, a small variance
\Var[D_{q}]
over the spectrum signals that this delocalization happens uniformly over all eigenstates. In the following we will focus on the first fractal dimension,
q=1
, which is sufficient for our purposes.

To gain a comprehensive picture of the dynamical landscape of the mixed-field Ising model (
30
), we analyze
D_{1}
in the full parameter space of our Hamiltonian.
To filter out potentially untypical eigenstates at the spectral edges, we focus on the bulk of the spectrum, and compute mean
\mathbb{E}[D_{1}]
and variance
\text{Var}[D_{1}]
over the inner
80\%
(in terms of energy) of the eigenstates.
To consolidate our analysis in Fig.
2
, where we consider the fractal dimension in the
n
-qubit
Z
-basis, we evaluate
D_{1}
in four additional random
n
-qubit Pauli bases. The results are shown in Fig.
12
for
n=8
.
The four columns correspond to the four chosen Pauli bases, given in the column titles.
For each column, we show the mean value
\mathbb{E}[D_{1}]
(top row) and variance
\text{Var}[D_{1}]
(bottom row), for the Ising Hamiltonian (
30
) in the parameter range
m\in\{0\}\cup[10^{-1},10^{1}]
,
h\in[10^{-1},10^{1}]
.
As before, we also include the value
m=0
, which corresponding to the (integrable) transverse-field Ising model.

As in Fig.
2
, the parameter domain
0.7\lesssim h/J\lesssim 2
,
0.2\lesssim m/J\lesssim 0.9
shows strong uniform eigenstate delocalization for all considered Pauli bases.
In this regime we observe a consistent maximization of
\mathbb{E}[D_{1}]
, which is accompanied by a drop of variance
\Var[D_{1}]
, signaling uniform properties of the eigenstates over the bulk of the spectrum.
The fact that this happens independently of the chosen basis, confirms that this region is indeed characterized by strong quantum chaotic behavior.
Consequently, we expect our model to align well with the ETH predictions in this parameter regime.
Note that the remaining parameter regions show different signatures for each chosen basis. This is expected. The fractal dimensions are, by construction, basis-dependent and we expect traces of this basis-dependence to be visible within the non-chaotic regimes of the model.
Physically, we can identify three
regular limits
of the Ising model (
30
);
m/J\rightarrow\infty
,
h/J\rightarrow\infty
or
m,h\rightarrow 0
.
In these limits the Hamiltonian is effectively given by only a single term.
In general, we expect ETH to be not applicable in the regular limits.
In the limit
m/J\rightarrow 0
, the mixed-field Ising model approaches the transverse field Ising model, which can be mapped to free fermions via the Jordan-Wigner transformation. This is an integrable (i.e. non-chaotic) model—however, it can exhibit critical behavior.

Table 1:

Summary of the Hamiltonian parameter configurations considered in our numerical experiments.
For each configuration, we give the label, the corresponding coordinates
h/J
and

, and the time step sizes for the considered values of

.
